2028 Should Be A Good Year

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
2028 Should Be A Good Year
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 6 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Patrick Fore / Unsplash
I. Blue skies ahead for earnings?
We are now seven years into our Roaring 2020s thesis, which remains
our base case for the rest of the decade, and it has played out very well
so far. When we first introduced the idea in August 2020, the prospect of
another Roaring '20s sounded a bit delusional. We argued that, as in the

### Page 3

1920s, rapidly proliferating technologies would drive a productivity boom
that would support stronger economic growth while containing inflation,
boosting real wages, widening profit margins, and lifting corporate
earnings.
Since the beginning of the current decade, the economy's resilience has
repeatedly defied the naysayers, as productivity growth has increased and
corporate earnings have continued to climb. With only three full years left
in the decade, one of the clearest manifestations of our Roaring 2020s

thesis has been what we call FEMO, or Fabulous Earnings Momentum.
FEMO is clearly visible in industry analysts' earnings expectations. They
currently expect S&P 500 operating earnings per share to rise to about
$364 per share in 2026 and $419 in 2027, implying growth of roughly 15%
in both years (chart). Toward the end of Q3 each year, we add another
year to our Earnings Squiggles framework. This year’s addition is 2028,
with earnings projected to rise another 17% that year to about $489 per

share.
For now, the analysts are more bullish than we are about earnings
prospects over the remainder of the decade. We are projecting $450 per
share in 2028, $475 in 2029, and $500 in 2030. If they are right, our S&P
500 target of 10,000 will be achieved well before the end of 2029!
The extended earnings boom is driven by strong revenue growth
expectations. Industry analysts expect S&P 500 revenues per share to

### Page 4

rise 12.7% in 2026, 9.2% in 2027, and another 8.3% in 2028 (chart).
Those expectations seem too exuberant to us.
Profit margins provide the other driver of the earnings story. Analysts
expect the S&P 500 profit margin to rise from 13.2% in 2025 to 15.7% this
year, 16.8% in 2027, and a record 18.1% in 2028 (chart). That is what we
would expect in our Roaring 2020s scenario, with stronger productivity
growth widening profit margins.
The earnings outlook is strong beyond the S&P 500 as well. Industry

analysts expect S&P 400 MidCap operating earnings per share to rise
roughly 19% in 2026, 14% in 2027, and 13% in 2028 (chart).

### Page 5

S&P 600 SmallCap earnings are expected to increase about 21% this
year, 16% in 2027, and 15% in 2028 (chart). That suggests FEMO is
broad-based.
II. Blue skies ahead for the economy?
(1) Consumer spending. The latest Redbook data provide yet another
sign of continued strength in consumer spending. Redbook same-store
sales rose 8.3% y/y during the week of October 2, well above the 5.8%
average for 2025 and underscoring the consumer’s continued resilience

(chart).

### Page 6

(2) Labor market. ADP estimates that private-sector payrolls rose 23,750
in the week through September 19, up from an upwardly revised 22,500
the prior week and marking the fifth consecutive acceleration in weekly job
gains (chart). It was also the strongest weekly increase in three months.
The ADP numbers for private industry payrolls make more sense to us
than the Bureau of Labor Statistics’ payroll employment data.
(3) Trade balance. The latest US trade data point to still strong US and

global economic growth. US imports rose 4.3% m/m and 23.6% y/y in
August, accelerating from July, while US exports increased 1.4% m/m and
12.3% y/y. Imports reached a $3.36 trillion annualized rate, versus $2.03
trillion for exports, leaving a merchandise trade deficit of $1.33 trillion
annualized (chart). The gap reflects exceptionally strong US demand for
imported goods.

### Page 7

Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 23.1% y/y
and 7.1%, respectively, in August (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global
economic growth.
Selected technology imports reached a record $56.6 billion in August
(chart). The AI buildout is highly import-intensive and requires large
volumes of semiconductors, servers, networking equipment, and other
hardware produced overseas.

clause.

1 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並 
營運「Terafab」評析

報告日期： 2025/09/19 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
新 聞 評 析 
新聞重點摘要： 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運「 Terafab 」。

一. 我們認為成案可能性極低  
我們認為台積電僅基於商業友好關係與馬斯克進行商討，但成案
的可能性極低，主因台積電表明過其晶圓廠的建置與營運都以
100% 自主擁有為主 。 雖然目前在德國的德勒斯登的ESMC 為合資
(台積電持股 70% 、博世持股 10% 、英飛凌持股 10% 、恩智浦持
股 10%) ，及在日本的熊本廠 JASM 為合資 (台積電持股 86.5% 、
Sony 持股  6.0% 、DENSO 持股 5.5% 、Toyota 持股 2.0%) ，但大
都因 2021 ～2022 年 COVID-19 期間車用晶圓持續供不應求，廠
商為了避免因半導體產能供貨不足而影響出貨的情況發生，紛紛
以能確保半導體晶圓供充足為優先，且後續接續 AI 需求蓬勃發
展，晶圓需求緊俏程度更加嚴重，各國紛紛力邀台積電到該國設
廠，日本及德國為汽車製造大國，因此，台積電於日本及德國的
設廠大都以車用的成熟製程或特殊製程為主 (僅 Sony 以 CMOS 影
像感測為主 )，在當地政府的建廠補貼下，也開放該國企業持有部
份股權。

我們認為本次傳言台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運
「 Terafab 」的技術層次將會拉高到最先進製程的水準，一旦
Terafab 開始營運後，將直接與台積電的先進製程產生衝突，因
此，我們認為成案的可能性極低。馬斯克建置的晶圓廠初期的想
法也是因為晶圓代工產能持續供不應求，馬斯克將建置自有的晶
圓廠滿足旗下 Tesla 車用晶片 、Optimus 機器人 、Dojo 訓練晶片、
AI 晶片、低軌衛星晶片，尤其因目前 AI 的競爭發展白熱化，晶片
的運算能力及設計複雜度都大幅提升，使用最先進的製程節點勢
在必行，因此，馬斯克先前也曾於 2026 年 4 月宣布將與 Intel 合
作 14A 製程，導入 Terafab 進行生產製造，由於 14A 將是 Intel
下一階段最先進的製程，顯見馬斯克對最先進的製程有強烈的需
求，其 Terafab 勢必以發展最先進製程為主，因此，若台積電與
馬斯克合作，勢必也要以其最先進的製程 2nm 為主，甚至下一世
代的 16A ，才能滿足馬斯克的期待，如此，台積電先進製程機密
可能有外洩風險，且若如馬斯克宣稱的 Terafab 初期產能 10 萬片

2 
/月，目前將達 100 萬片 /月，屆時晶圓代工產能將有過剩問題，因
為台積電 2025 年約當 12 吋晶圓的出貨量約 1,500 萬片 ，Terafab
若達 100 萬片 /月(年產能 1,200 萬片 )，將是一個龐大的產能的䆁
出。

目前台積電 2nm+3nm+5nm 佔總營收比重約 66% ，由於 ASP 相
對較高，我們推估其出貨量僅佔總出貨量的 35% ，毛利率也高於
平均水準 (雖然 2nm 仍在進行良率調整，尚未達公司目標，但其於
4Q25 開始量產，僅僅花兩個季度就已經開始賺錢 )，在 AI 需求強
勁的帶動下，台積電也非常極積的擴充 3nm 的產能，甚至將 5nm
的設備改機成 3nm ，顯見得 5nm 以下為台積電最賺錢的製程節
點，一旦與馬斯克合作 5nm 以下的製程節點，製程機密可能外流
的風險將遠高於未來可能接單的利益。考量台積電持續為 AI 浪潮
下的主要受惠者，先進製程、先進封裝同步大幅優於競爭對手，
預估台積電 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 為 108.1/142.76 元，投資建
議為買進，目標價 3141 元(22X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

E Ink (8069.TWO): E-paper application continues to expand; expect 
modest consumer electronics mix; Aug beat; Neutral
30 September 2026 | 3:59PM HKT
We remain positive on growing e-paper applications across e-readers, e-notes, and 
e-labels. However, consumer electronics end-demand could remain modest amid 
high memory costs. We expect E Ink’s consumer electronics (e-readers, e-notes) to 
decline 13% YoY in 2026E (vs. +6% YoY in 2025), and recover back to +12-16% YoY 
in 2027E-28E; on IoT applications (ESL and Signage), we expect revenue to grow at a 
+32% CAGR in 2026E-28E (vs. +22% YoY in 2025), driven by rising adoption on 
savings of labour costs, electricity, and paper, digitalization trend, and growing ESG 
awareness. Signage new products could also drive revenue growth with high ASPs, 
despite lower GMs considering in module format to accelerate customers’ adoption. 
Our target P/E multiple declines to 11x (vs. 13x previous TP implied 2027E P/E) on 
consumer electronics sector de-rating under high memory costs. Our new 12m TP is 
NT$142 (vs. NT$153 previously). Maintain Neutral.

3-month revenues preview: We expect a sequential increase in revenues through 
Sep-Dec, driven by e-labels, Signage ramp up, and better consumer electronics 
seasonality. The ongoing digitalization should support e-paper adoption; E Ink’s 
e-labels further penetrated in Canada, serving Sobeys (retail) to deploy ESLs across 
300 to 350 stores over the next 18 months (link). Overall, we expect 3Q26E / 4Q26E 
revenues to grow at +0% / +16% QoQ on the mix of modest consumer electronics 
end-demand under high memory costs, and ongoing e-labels adoption and Signage 
shipment ramp up.

### Page 2

Earnings revision

We factor in 2Q26 and Aug results and revise up our 2026E-28E net income by 15% / 
10% / 8% mainly on higher gross margins, given better product mix (e-labels mainly 
delivered via e-paper while consumer electronics mainly via module, carry a lower GM). 
Overall, we expect 2026E revenues growth at +13% YoY (vs. +12% YoY in 2025A), driven 
by e-labels growing application and adoption, which we model to grow +47% YoY (vs. 
+22% YoY in 2025A), while we also expect a decline in consumer electronics under high 
memory costs (-13% YoY vs. +6% YoY in 2025). We continue to expect revenue growth in 
2027E-28E driven by (1) rising color e-notes / e-readers enlarging size, (2) more stores 
adopting e-labels to save operation costs with growing outdoor signage. On GM, we 
increase our forecasts in 2026E-28E to reflect 2Q26 beat on better product mix.

### Page 3

Valuation

We continue to derive our 12m TP based on a near-term P/E. We derive our target P/E 
via E Ink’s avg. 12m forward PEG ratio since 2017. With largely the same PEG ratio of 
0.5x, 22% forward year earnings growth (vs. 26% previously), our new target P/E is at 
11x (vs. 13x our TP implied 2027E P/E previously). The new target P/E of 11x is largely in 
line with the company’s avg.-1stv. P/E, reflecting the consumer electronics sector 
de-rating under high memory costs. Our new 12m target price is NT$142 (vs. NT$153 
previously). Maintain Neutral.

### Page 4

Price target methodology and risks - E Ink 
Valuation methodology: We are Neutral rated on E Ink. Our 12m target price for E Ink is 
based on a near-term P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. Our 12-m 
TP of NT$142 is based on a target P/E multiple of 11x forward year EPS (2027E). Our 
target P/E multiple of 11x is derived from the company’s avg. PEG since 2017 (0.5x), as 
we expect macro uncertainties to reduce the visibility of e-paper adoption, while we 
remain positive in the long term: technology upgrades (colour, larger sizes) to unlock the 
e-paper market potential. 
Upside / Downside risks: (1) Faster- / slower-than-expected e-readers / e-notes 
penetration rate, (2) Faster- / slower-than-expected ESL adoption rate, (3) Fiercer- / 
healthier-than-expected competition, which could be driven by competition from other 
technology gadgets (e.g. AI / AR glasses, AI PCs, tablets, smart watches, etc.) or new 
entrants in e-paper industry.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

Continued Capex spree underpins strong 
business outlook

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 800.00 TWD | Price: 703.00 TWD

YTD equipment buys tracks 82% of its '26 capex guidance 
ASE has filed 152 equipment buys and 101 facility construction announcements through 
late September. The spending on equipment YTD has reached NT$273bn, tracking 82% 
of its '26 capex guidance of US$10.5bn (has revised up from US$8.5bn) not yet 
considering their land and facility purchase. The equipment buy should mainly be for 
2.5D packaging and chip probing, reflecting accelerated capacity expansion amid strong 
demand for its LEAP business from its own project wins and its foundry partner’s 
outsourcing. To secure sufficient facility infrastructure to house additional capacity, ASE 
has announced NT$112bn of spending YTD on facility and clean room construction 
contracts, with these projects expected to progressively complete through 2027-2028.  
Solid sales/margins across the board sponsor heavy capex 
Following ASE’s July announcements to acquire new lands and facilities in Kaohsiung and 
Taoyuan, the company announced another NT$2.15bn facility acquisition in Singapore to 
support the future testing expansion needs in its Singapore subsidiary as its major 
customers request supply base diversification. Despite rising depreciation from heavy 
capex investments and additional funding needs, we forecast ASE’s IC ATM sales to 
+37% CAGR from ‘26-28 while GMs should improve to 33.8%/37% in ‘27/28 (from 
28.3% in 2026) on solid packaging and testing demand for AI chipsets (BofAe LEAP 
sales US$4/10/13bn in 2026/27/28) and fast improving mainstream business outlook.  
Decent earnings support re-rating despite funding needs 
We keep Buy on ASE with NT$800 PO on 20x 2H27-1H28 P/E and maintain EPS for 
'26/27/28E at NT$18/31/45. While additional funding may be required to finance its 
aggressive spending on expansion and R&D projects, we believe the decent customer 
commitment and ROE of 25-30%+ from LEAP business should justify the funding costs.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$800 per share (US$51 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

Document 074: 261005_daiwa_GUC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_GUC.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Global Unichip (GUC) reported September revenue which closed its 3Q26 top-
line 19% above market expectations thanks to its stronger-than-expected turnkey revenue 
run-rate during the quarter, likely attributable to more capacity support from its key foundry 
helping GUC meet strong demand from customers for AI projects. We see upside risk to 
our/consensus 3Q26 bottom-line estimates but are currently comfortable with our 4Q26 
forecasts despite the 3Q26 revenue beat likely dwarfing QoQ growth for 4Q26. We have a 
Buy (1) rating on the counter and will review our model in due course post its quarterly 
results release.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 top-line beating expectations. GUC released September revenue of TWD7.1bn, 
up 22% MoM (+98% YoY, see chart below), which closed its 3Q26 top-line at 
TWD18.8bn, up 35% QoQ (+118% YoY) for a new record. 3Q26 revenue beat our and 
consensus estimates both by 19% each thanks to its turnkey business (ie, mass-
production) which contributed TWD16.7bn for 89% of total and beat our estimate by 
22%. We attribute the beat to likely its key foundry continuously unleashing extra 
capacity helping GUC meet the strong customer AI demand during the quarter.

• Quarterly results preview and outlook. We expect GUC to have earned 3Q26 net 
profit of TWD1.7bn, varying not much from the consensus estimate, yet given the top-
line beat, we now see upside risk to the market expectations. As for 4Q26, our top-line 
and bottom-line forecasts are currently 10% and 26% above the consensus, 
respectively, making us comfortable with our current forecasts since we see the 3Q26 
beat being driven by increased foundry capacity allocation for some shipment pull-in, 
thus dwarfing 4Q26’s QoQ growth on intact AI demand. As flagged in our previous note 
(Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to growth, 5 August 2026), we continue to 
expect GUC to be a key beneficiary from the strong AI ASIC ramps, including AI CPU 
and accelerators.

Recommendation 
We rate GUC Buy (1) with a 12M TP of TWD 5,125 on our 4-quarter forward PER target of 
63x. The stock performed well recently which we see as largely pricing in the 3Q26 top-line 
beat, therefore only suggesting investors accumulate only on any technical pullback. Key 
downside risks are less foundry support and margin execution.

 Unleashed capacity adding to upside risk

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H/A)

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track 
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside

### Page 2

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside
Migration in raw materials of CCL
Spec advancements in resin more debatable
Major raw materials for building CCL (copper clad laminate) include cloth, resin, and
copper foil. Glass cloth serves as grounding and offers support to the CCL and PCB
(printed circuit board) structure, and it is immersed in resins built on different in-house
chemical recipes by CCL makers to obtain PP (prepreg). Afterwards, CCL makers

add/laminate the copper foil on this intermediate product (PP), to form the CCL supplied
to PCB customers. Across the previous generations (Exhibit 2), we have seen rising
penetration of PPE/PPO (polyphenylene ether/oxide) gradually replacing FR-4/ epoxy
resins throughout M4-M8 grade CCL, while hydrocarbon resin gradually gains traction
from M8-grade CCL. Yet, there is a debate on PTFE-based (polytetrafluoroethylene) CCL
vs hydrocarbon resin as the CCL grade advances into M9/M10.

As illustrated in Exhibit 4, we see a more diversified path of resin upgrades from M8-
grade CCL, while the advancement in copper foil and glass cloth seems to be more
certain. In general, we note that the mix of PPO vs hydrocarbon used is roughly 2:1 in
M8-grade CCL, and such proportion reverses to 1:2 in M9-grade CCL. Yet, the broader
range of resin options could potentially alter the competitive dynamics within the CCL
industry, since different vendors are promoting (or have edge) in different types of CCL

solutions. Or in other words, the likely penetration by PTFE-based solutions (pushed
mainly by NVIDIA) will favor the suppliers with experiences in handling relevant products
(though smaller market size such as base station), while, at the same time, it might
curtail the headroom for major Taiwanese suppliers, including EMC and TUC (Taiwan
Union), to grow.
PPO/BMI vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9; hydrocarbon vs PTFE in M9/M10
In our view, hydrocarbon resin exhibits an ultra-low dielectric constant (Dk) of 2.2-2.6

and a dissipation factor (Df) reaching 0.001-0.005, as low as 0.0005 at high frequencies
(e.g., 10 GHz and above), thanks to its non-polar molecular backbone. However, there are
drawbacks to hydrocarbon resin, including rather limited flexibility and high brittleness
after curing, which necessitate the mix with ductile polymers such as PPO or BMI
(bismaleimide) to improve the toughness (peel strength). Nonetheless, the performance
in Dk/Df would be reduced after adding PPO/BMI to hydrocarbon resin, which makes the

solution not that favorable in meeting customers’ demand/requirement.
Within hydrocarbon resin, we note there are two major advanced versions, including ODV
(oxidative vulcanization; lower-end) and BCB (benzocyclobutene; higher-end), and key
suppliers for the latter include Dow Chemical and Asahi Kasei. In terms of Df, <0.0008
can be reached by BCB type of hydrocarbon resin based on the standard of M9-grade
CCL, and the number would hit <0.0005 based on the standard of M9+/M10-grade. By

CCL suppliers, we note EMC (Elite Material) adopts hydrocarbon/BMI resin in M9-grade
CCL, while Panasonic blends PPO or BMI (with hydrocarbon) in M9 (vs PPO/hydrocarbon
in M7/M8). Shengyi Technology also adopts PPO resin in M9-grade offering.
Given the disadvantages (discussed above) of PPO resin, some CCL providers
alternatively also adopt mPPO (modified PPO; Exhibit 5) by blending the PPO with other
resins (e.g., epoxy methacrylate, styrene). By doing so, the overall dielectric and thermal

properties of the composite are enhanced, and the reduced molecular weight improves
solubility in solvents like toluene and MEK (methyl ethyl ketone). This in turn enables the
resin to thermoset, and it becomes more suitable for CCL processing. By application, we
believe it is adopted in the LPU (language processing unit) and UBB (universal
baseboard) under Vera Rubin platform, 1.6T networking switch, and will also be
potentially (competing with PTFE-based solutions) adopted in the backplane.

### Page 3

Similar to what we have seen in PPO/PPE vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9, we believe
hydrocarbon and PTFE will coexist and complement each other in M9/M10-grade CCL,
while the sheer adoption on one side is unlikely, given 1) different consideration on
cost/performance by different customers; and 2) the difficulty in sourcing across resin
and the adjacent equipment in producing different types of CCL (e.g. vacuum hot press
machine). Meanwhile, we note that PPO/PPE cannot be paired with PTFE given the

conflicts in characteristics.
In addition to the specialized plasma treatment, and long lead time in pressing machine
(operating under high temperature) with high costs, one of the other major drawbacks to
PTFE is the lack of stiffness in texture which makes it hard to be processed under PCB
drilling/lamination. Thus, SiO2 (silicon dioxide) is used as a filler (or with increasing
consumption vs that in M7/M8-grade CCL) to fill the resin matrix, and this helps reduce

the thermal expansion coefficient (CTE) and enhance the mechanical strength of the
composites. Elsewhere, a lack of adhesion to copper foil is the other key technological
challenge when processing PTFE-based solutions, and this requires the help from
hydrocarbon resin (to enhance the adhesion).
In short, the pursuit of more advanced materials like PTFE/hydrocarbon will
simultaneously incur other technological challenges, which, in our view, makes relevant

solutions to become more versatile (to solve the associated challenges). In Exhibit 6, we
present the promoted solutions of M9/M10-grade CCL in the market, and we compare
different metrics, including signal loss, mechanical strength, processibility, and costs.
Given the low penetration by M9-grade CCL currently (evidenced by single-digit% of
industry leader EMC’s revenue), we believe none of these solutions has become
mainstream. In other words, we deem the ongoing development and qualification/testing

results worth monitoring, and these include the feedback from board, ODM (original
design manufacturer) and end-customers’ levels. In our view, the most imminent (and
perhaps critical) one will likely be the solution adopted by Rubin Ultra switch board and
backplane (if any), which could be finally confirmed by end-2026/early-2027.

Exhibit 5: A comparison between PPO and mPPO 
mPPO carries enhanced processability and reactivity when compared with PPO 
Characteristics/type of 
resin PPO mPPO 
Dk Inherently low. Values for PPO-based composites are typically in the range of 2.1 - 3.5.  Retains low Dk, with high-performance grades achieving tightly controlled values, e.g., 2.8 - 3.0 for M9 grade. 
Df Inherently very low. Values for PPO-based systems can be as low as 0.0006. Significantly reduced and optimized. Ranges from 0.004 – 0.008 for M6/M7 grades down to <0.002 (typically 0.0015–0.002) for ultra-low loss M9 grades. 
Processability Limited. High melt viscosity and lack of reactivity make it difficult to process.  Better. Low molecular weight and reactive end-groups enable good solubility, compatibility with CCL manufacturing, and thermosetting cross-linking. 
Reactivity Low. Lacks sufficient reactive groups to cross-link effectively with other resins. High. Functionalized end-groups (e.g., styrene, methacrylate) allow it to chemically bond with other components in the resin formulation. 
Source: BofA Global Research

Clearer upgrades in glass cloth and copper foil  
While the upgrades in resin system appear to be more debatable with a versatility in 
solutions, we witness a firmer roadmap advancement in glass cloth and copper foil. For 
glass cloth, (see reports) Nittobo shared the view of 100% demand growth in NER this 
year (FY3/27), followed by another c60% growth next year. As for copper foil, Mitsui 
Kinzoku also revised up the mix of HVLP4 and above products in 1H26 (within very 
smooth profile copper foil) to 45% (was 40%), and the figure is expected to reach 60% 
in 2H26. On pricing, we note that Mitsui Kinzoku also mentioned it would try pursuing a 
higher ASP in product segments where demand remains strong, while there is a higher 
processing fee (Exhibit 7) charged by copper foil suppliers in the high-end copper foil 
market. All-in-all, we see positive indications to the high-end CCL pricing, given that the 
CCL makers will try to pass through the inflated costs (due to stronger demand and the 
premium in processing fee) to the product ASP.

Ongoing spec migration on multiple fronts into CY27-28 
We see a clear upgrade cycle for CCL materials and PCB production technique across 
networking switches and AI (artificial intelligence) accelerators, including GPUs (graphic 
processing unit) and ASICs (application specific integrated circuits). In our view, the spec 
advancement (Exhibit 8) is primarily driven by faster data rates and increasingly 
stringent signal-integrity requirements. For NVIDIA, GB200/GB300 largely adopt M8-
grade CCL, with low-Dk1/2 glass cloth and HVLP2/3 copper foil. The transition to VR200 
drives further CCL specification upgrades toward M8+/M9-grade, along with the 
adoption of HVLP4 copper foil in VR200 compute board and midplane, while LPU adopts 
Q cloth with less signal loss (vs low Dk2).  
While NVIDIA tends to place greater emphasis on glass cloth upgrades, ASICs and 
networking switches are more focused on copper foil migration. Among ASICs, we 
expect the CCL spec for Google TPU (tensor processing unit) v8 to migrate to M8+

based on low Dk2 cloth and HVLP3 copper foil (vs M7-grade CCL with low Dk1 and
HVLP2 for TPU v7), followed by M9-grade adoption in TPU v9 based on HVLP4 copper
foil (with either low Dk2 or Q-cloth). As for the Trainium chips by Amazon, there is a
more visible upgrade in copper foil (vs glass cloth) taking place in Trainium 3 (2026),
while its OAM (open accelerator module) in Trainium 4 (2027) will likely be upgraded to
M8-grade CCL based on low Dk2 cloth. Simultaneously, we also expect a migration

toward HDI (high-density interconnect) from HLC (high-layer count), with regard to the
PCB production for the UBB (universal baseboard) in TPU v9 and Trainium 4.

Along with the data-rate migration toward 800G and beyond (from 100/200G) for optical
module, PCB structure evolves from conventional tenting design toward more
complicated mSAP/SAP (semi-additive process) with higher layer count, enabling finer
L/S (line/space) and smaller pitch (Exhibit 9). To elaborate, L/S of 3.2T optical module
should shrink materially to 15/15μm from 60/60μm at 800G, and it was 125/125um for

100G generation. Simultaneously, the layer count would also increase to up to layers for
3.2T products, vs 10-layer in 400G generation.
Amid the backdrop of rapid spec migration, major PCB suppliers are accelerating
capacity expansion to capture the burgeoning demand in high-end optical module
(Exhibit 10), and these include Avary under ZDT (Zhen Ding Technology), Founder, SCC
(Shennan Circuits), Unimicron and Compeq. While Unimicron remains the biggest

supplier of optical module PCB (printed circuit board) in 2H26, we flag Avary and SCC
could potentially become the other major suppliers in 2027, given their more aggressive
capacity increase.

Expect further ASP uplift driven by mix & cost inflation 
We expect CCL (for PCB) names’ revenue growth to be fueled by continuous capacity 
expansion and ASP uplifts, and these include EMC, TUC, Shengyi Tech and Kingboard 
Laminates among our coverage. The ASP increase, in our view, will be driven by both 
price hike on a like-for-like basis (given the cost push) and mix improvements to higher 
CCL grades, and we note the former/latter represent bigger boost to the near-
term/long-term ASP. To elaborate, the difference in near-term vs long-term boost can 
be explained by 1) a shorter production cycle time of less than 1-2 weeks by CCL makers 
which normally carry less than a quarter of inventory; and 2) a continuous spec 
advancement in CCL material grade given the requirement in data-transmission.  
In Exhibit 11-13, we decipher the revenue growth by different CCL names based on the 
planned capacity expansion and ASP uplift, as well as the assumption of 95%/90%/90% 
capacity utilization rate by EMC/TUC/Shengyi Tech in 2028. In our view, the implied ASP 
growth of 4%/11%/18% by respective names look conservative, and we see room for 
further upside in the case of continuous cost inflation from the raw materials (e.g. glass 
cloth, copper foil). Further, we believe a smaller magnitude of ASP increase by EMC (vs 
TUC/Shengyi Tech) in 2028 can be explained by its already significant exposure to high-
end CCL market in 2027. For reference, we present the CCL ASP by each grade with the 
figure for M4-grade indexed to 100 in Exhibit 20, and the gap of ASP by each grade 
remains quite meaningful, in our view.  
Regarding the CCL mix by grade, we break down the revenue contribution by different 
grades to each company throughout 2025-28, and we compare the numbers to their 
gross margins in Exhibit 14-19. When comparing the gross margin, we carve out the 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL, given that M6-grade is already in supply-
demand balance (or slightly oversupply) situation from late-2025.

### Page 8

Among the three names, we expect EMC to remain the industry leader with 
73%/85%/92% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 2026/27/28, which helps 
strengthen its gross margin to 35.8%/36.7% in 2027/28 (vs 34% in 2026). TUC will 
follow suit with 43%/47%/53% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 
2026/27/28, with gross margin edging toward 32.6%/33.7% in 2027/28 (vs 30.4% in 
2026). As for Shengyi Tech, we expect 28%/35%/57% revenue mix from M7-grade and 
above CCL in 2026/27/28, and its gross margin (in CCL business) is expected to inch up 
to 31%/31.8 in 2027/28 (vs 30.7% in 2026).

 BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Regarding the ASP uplifts by firm in 2027, we further decode into the factors of cost 
inflation and mix enhancement as shown in Exhibit 21. To simply put, we expect a 
smaller boost to EMC (vs TUC/Shengyi Tech) from cost inflation of raw materials and 
the corresponding pass-through, given its relatively lower revenue exposure to low-/mid-
end CCL market, where CCL makers pass through the cost more frequently upon 
squeezed margin profile. Yet, on the other hand, EMC (in terms of ASP) is expected to 
benefit more from the enhanced mix in 2027, thanks to the further ramp of M8/M8.5-
grade CCL as well as the penetration increase of M9 and PTFE-based solutions.  
Year-to-date, we believe there is already 40%+/20%+ price hike across low-/high-end 
CCL, and we do not rule out the likelihood of further price increase given the (see report) 
expectations of continuously rising copper price into late-2026 and 2027 (Exhibit 22). 
There was a price hike of VSP (very smooth profile) foil, effective from July 2026 by 
Mitsui Kinzoku given the demand strength and likely to be followed suit by a Taiwanese 
supplier (Co-Tech), based on our checks with supply chain. On the glass cloth side, we 
note the potential pricing upside from low Dk2 cloth given the supply shortage, while the 
price of low-end E-glass cloth (such as 7628 type) remains on the uptrend (Exhibit 23) 
and may reach CNY15-16 (per msq) by end-2026. In our view, all of these point to a 
benign pricing outlook for CCL makers.  
To pinpoint the benefits from price increase by CCL makers, we conduct a scenario 
analysis to each name’s 2027 bottom-line as shown in Exhibit 24. In short, we expect 
1.2-1.6% upside to the net income estimates coming from every 1ppt price hike, if such 
price increase purely reflects cost inflation. On the other hand, we expect 3.3-5.1% 
upside to the net income estimates, if the price hike is purely driven a supply shortage 
(which enables CCL makers to raise the ASP proactively). Further on Shengyi Tech, we 
assume that its PCB business (Shengyi Electronics) can also pass on the cost increase 
(from CCL side), with same magnitude to its ASP.  
For Kingboard Laminates, its vertical integration significantly enhances its earnings 
sensitivity to price hikes, as most of its key raw materials are produced in-house. 
Notably, price hikes in glass fiber cloth can translate almost entirely into profit, given in-
house production of yarn. Our analysis suggests that 100% increase in CCL prices could 
drive c.28ppt gross margin expansion for KBL, and more than 200% growth at the EPS 
level in 2026. This assumes that future price hikes are primarily driven by cloth 
tightness, while input costs for copper foil and resin remain relatively stable.

Sector view: stay positive on industry leaders  
We stay constructive on the CCL for PCB area, given the ongoing spec upgrades and 
robust demand growth into 2027-28, and we believe the worries from CPO (co-packaged 
optics) proliferation are either overstated or distant, as the mass adoption of CPO to 
replace NPO (near-packaged optics) or pluggable optical modules will not take place until 
2029/30, at earliest.  
Currently, we see extended lead-time (of production) toward 2-3 months by high-end 
CCL makers in 2H26 (vs 1-2 months seen in 1H26), while low/mid-end suppliers’ lead 
time is also extended to 1-2 months (7-10 days before). We also believe the current 
valuation by EMC (21-22x 2027E P/E) and TUC (17-18x 2027E P/E) looks compelling, 
given that the numbers do not seem lofty when compared with the historical trading 
range of 1) 15-20x before mid-2023; and 2) normally above 20x after mid-2023 when 
the strong demand from AI server starts to emerge. As for Kingboard Laminates, it is 
traded at 8x 2027E P/E, below historical average of 10x. 
Among our coverage, we highlight Buy-rated EMC, given its technology leadership in 
high-end CCL area along with diversified customer profile across GPU and ASIC 
accounts, as well as TUC, given its (see report) aggressive capacity expansion into 2027 
and beyond. We also highlight Buy rating on Doosan Corp and Kingboard Laminates, 
given the former’s solid position in NVIDIA GPU and Amazon switch, as well as the 
latter’s robust earnings expansion amid CCL price hike and rising high-end contribution 
starting 2027. As for Shengyi Tech, we remain relatively conservative, given its relatively 
slower expansion in CCL capacity along with a milder growth in the PCB business.

EMC (2383 TT; Buy; PO NT$8,200) 
We reckon EMC’s potential share loss in Rubin Ultra switch board (tray), given the 
competition in PTFE-based (hybrid) solutions (vs M9-Q), as well as the ongoing debate 
between PTFE and quartz-glass/hydrocarbon resin solutions into M10-grade CCL. We 
also note that M9-Q (where EMC has more notable technology leadership) is likely to be 
adopted in backplane by NVIDIA, no matter if it will initially be seen in Rubin Ultra or 
Feynman generation. Net-net, we believe the impact on bottom-line (if any) from 
aforementioned headwinds should be confined to LSD (low-single-digit)%, considering 
1) 80-90k (per month) of relevant demand by NVIDIA’s switch board vs EMC’s capacity 
of ~6/9mn in 2026/27; and 2) the demand backfill from other high-end projects across 
AMD and other ASIC customers.  
Looking ahead, we see further expansion plan beyond 1H28 as a catalyst, if it is 
announced. In addition to the CCL for PCB business, we note that substrate CCL 
business boasts at least 50-60% GM (gross margin), and EMC could expand relevant 
capacity to 1.2mn sheet /month which could help expedite its market-share gain toward 
~5% (vs 1-2% in 2025). We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/11%/14% after penciling in 
stronger revenue growth and gross margin profile, while our PO stays at NT$8,200 as 
we apply a lower P/E multiple of 30x (was 34x), with the same valuation period of 2H27-
1H28E, mainly reflecting the overhang from competition in PTFE-based solutions.

TUC (6274 TT; Buy; PO NT$2,600) 
Looking into 2027, we flag multiple positives from spec advancements for TUC, 
including 1) the potentially partial adoption of M9-grade CCL by 1.6T networking switch, 
where TUC’s solution is based on low Dk3 glass cloth; 2) the upgrade to M8-grade CCL 
for LEO (low earth orbit); and 3) the migration toward M8-grade material by Vera CPU 
(central processing unit). Meanwhile, we share the view that hybrid solution from PTFE-
based CCL may last toward M10-grade. Demand from high-end optical module (800G 
and above) could have remained underestimated at current stage, as we believe relevant 
TAM (total addressable market) for CCL could reach NT$30bn+, which represents close 
to 50% of TUC’s total revenue in 2026. In terms of Thailand operations, we believe it 
could turn breakeven or profitable from 3Q26, thanks to the smooth ramp of 
capacity/output to pair with the robust demand growth outlook from customers.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 7%/11%/7% after baking in the assumptions of stronger 
revenue growth and slightly enhanced GM profile as well as operating leverage. Our PO 
stays at NT$2,600, based on a lower P/E multiple of 27x (was 30.5x) and an unchanged 
valuation period of 2H27-1H28E. We attribute the cut in valuation multiple to the likely 
growing traction by PTFE-based solutions that serve as other alternatives in the high-
end CCL market.

Shengyi Technology (600183 CH; U/P; PO CNY99) 
We believe revenue contribution from PTFE-based CCL could reach ~10% based on the 
assumption of US$300-400 ASP per piece, and we expect the ramp of such business to 
bolster the overall margin profile thanks to ~50% GM by relevant solutions. Yet we also 
note that the firm’s capacity for M6-grade and above CCL remains relatively limited at 
~10% mix currently, and further capacity ramp will be dependent on the progress in 
Thailand and Songshan Lake (for M7-M9), after its Jiangxi phase 2 capacity became fully 
loaded after 1H26.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 29%/21%/18% after baking in 2Q26 results and the 
assumptions of stronger revenue growth and margin profiles, which also reflect its 
further share gain in NVIDIA’s AI server system/platforms. Our PO goes to CNY99 (from 
CNY83), same based on 24x 2027E P/E, and the volatility risk rating is changed to C 
from B.

Valuation discussion  
Both Buy-rated EMC and TUC are expected to post stronger EPS growth toward 2028 
along with a higher dividend yield. However, they trade at lower P/E multiples when 
compared with peers’ average in CCL industry. As for P/B multiples, the premium 
valuation by these two names, in our view, can be explained by much higher ROE 
expected in 2027-28. On the other hand, our more conservative view on Shengyi can be 
supported by its more stretched valuation (for both P/E and P/B) vs milder EPS growth 
and lower dividend yield, when compared with the peers’ average. Overall, we believe the 
(see report) overhang from CPO proliferation is already largely reflected in the valuation 
of CCL names, which we believe also renders an attractive opportunity for EMC/TUC, 
considering their solid earnings growth ahead fueled by continuous mix upgrades and 
more aggressive capacity expansion.

Price objective basis & risk 
Doosan Corp (DOSAF) 
Our PO of W1,800,000 is derived from SOTP valuation, which implies a fair market cap 
of W32tn = W20tn for core businesses (based on 20x 2027-28E P/E, which is in line with

### Page 22

the average of CCL peers) + W12tn in listed equity investments (Doosan Enerbility, 
Doosan Robotics, and Oricom) + net debt (breakeven) + other investments (W0.25tn, 
mostly non-listed affiliates). Our W1,800,000 PO implies 8x 2027-28E P/B, which 
appears much higher than the 2023-25 average of 4x, as well as a relatively high P/E 
(31x based on 2027-28E EPS) versus other Korea Holdco companies (5-10x). Key reason 
behind our relatively higher fair multiples compared to its historical average and its 
peers should be Doosan's rapidly growing CCL business thanks to its dominant market 
share position within NVIDIA.

Upside risks: (1) further market share gains in high-end CCL for major US hyperscalers, 
(2) a more favorable CCL pricing environment driven by sustained demand growth and 
tight industry supply, (3) incremental demand opportunities from emerging technologies 
such as co-packaged optics (CPO) and other networking equipment, and (4) faster-than-
expected capacity ramp-up.

Downside risks: (1) a slowdown in AI infrastructure spending or cuts to US hyperscaler 
capex, which could weaken demand for high-end CCL used in AI servers and networking 
equipment, (2) intensifying competition and pricing pressure in the advanced CCL 
market, and (3) potential delays in Doosan's CCL capacity expansion. 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kingboard Laminates (KGBLF) 
We set our PO at HK$65, on 13x 2H26-1H27E P/E. 13x is around +0.5SD of KBL's 
historical trading range, and matches with its last earnings upcycle in 2021 (led by e-
glass price hike) while also reflects rising mix from high-end AI-related deliveries.

Downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) better-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) slower-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) slower-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) slower-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) stronger-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) faster-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales. 
Shengyi (DGUNF) 
Our PO of CNY99 is based on 24x 2027E P/E, close to the mid-to-high-end range of the 
historical average of 10-35x in 2017-2024. In our view, such valuation multiple is backed 
by operating margin and ROE of 23% and 37% in 2026-28E (vs 11% and 12% in 2023-
25).

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) better macro conditions, (2) faster 5G 
deployment, (3) less-intensive competition, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
changes.

### Page 23

Downside risks are: (1) worse macro conditions, (2) slower 5G deployment, (3) more 
severe competition, and (4) unfavorable FX and raw material cost changes. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

TFT-LCD Panel Price Outlook
for Oct-26: TVs, Monitors and
NBs Flat MoM
While panel makers plan to raise the panel prices to mitigate
cost pressure, the coinciding softer seasonality in panel order
momentum makes this look a bit challenging, so negotiations
are undergoing. Contribution from class opportunities in
advanced packaging should only come in  2028 by earliest.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

75" 4K 65" 4K 55" FHD 50" FHD 43" FHD 32" HD
Source:                         Trendforce, Morgan Stanley Research
TV panel prices to stay  flat MoM in October: Mainstream 32", 43", 55", 65" and 75"
TV panel prices are expected to stay flat for the month of October. While panel
makers had previously notified their brands customers of potential price hikes from
4Q26 to mitigate elevated component costs, we believe that negotiations are
ongoing, as TV panel order momentum might slow down in the coming months on

seasonality and TV brands could reduce orders on higher panel prices. On the other
hand, panel makers continue to demonstrate good discipline, since Chinese panel
makers are committed to annual maintenance during the October holiday to control
output. We think it seems more likely that we will see TV panel price hikes toward
late 4Q26 when TV brands start to restock for CNY promotions. Our base-case
assumption is for the TV panel prices  to be flat QoQ in 4Q26, on a quarterly average

basis.
IT panel prices to remain flat MoM in October: Monitor and notebook panel prices
are expected to be flat MoM during the month. Both applications continue to  see
slower demand in  2H26 post the earlier restocking  in 1H26 amid concerns about
increasing component costs. That said, panel makers are under cost pressure and
reluctant to given more price concessions. On a quarterly average basis, we expect
IT panel prices to remain flat QoQ in 4Q26.

Risk/reward  less attractive: We maintain the view that the current valuation of
Taiwanese panel makers, AUO (2409.TW) at 2.0x P/B and Innolux (3481.TW) at 1.7x
P/B, have already baked in the expectations of glass in advanced packaging for
revenue contribution in 2028. However, some supply-chain players think this might
take longer, and there seem to be more suppliers from different supply chains vying
for this opportunity. In China, while expectations are also building for advanced

packaging, we have a    relative preference for BOE (000725.SZ; 1.4x P/B) over TCL
(000100.SZ; 1.7x P/B), on its scale  and faster progress in glass core substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We believe current valuations of 1.5-2.0x P/B have already priced in expectations
of glass in advanced packaging from 2H28 for the panel makers.
Nonetheless,  the risk in commercialization and competition remain key to watch.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
TV Panel Prices Trended Down in 3Q26
TV panel prices started to edge up at the beginning of this year, rising 1% MoM in January,
2% MoM in February and 2% MoM in March. April, May and June were largely flat, while
July was down 2% MoM, August was down 1% MoM, and September was flat MoM,   in line
with our expectation that TV panel prices would start trending down from 3Q26.  That
said, we do not expect a significant decline as supply-side discipline should provide

downside support.

Below-seasonal Panel Shipments in 2H26
Over the past decade, TV panel shipments have typically been split 49%:51% between 1H
and 2H. In 2026, we expect the mix to skew toward 1H, as TV brands restocked ahead of
major promotional events, including the Winter Olympics in February, the FIFA World Cup
in June-July, and China's 618 shopping festival. We see no clear evidence that these events

will materially boost global TV demand, but they have historically shifted shipment timing
across quarters. As a result, we expect the restocking momentum seen since the start of
the year to fade, with panel order momentum slowing in 2H26.
Display Fab Utilization  ~85% in 3Q26
Stronger TV panel shipments supported display fab utilization of ~80% in 4Q24 and 82%
in 1Q25. Utilization declined to 78% in 2Q25 as brands adopted a more cautious stance
following earlier pull-ins and as the impact of China's trade-in program moderated.

Utilization rebounded to 80-85% in 3Q25 as brands restocked for year-end promotions
and remained at similar levels in 4Q25 despite seasonal softness as well as in 1Q26.
Industry utilization stayed broadly flat QoQ, at 80-85% in 2Q26, reflecting continued
supply discipline, followed by a slight uptick in 3Q26.
Over the medium to long term, we continue to believe the display industry is undergoing
structural change. Chinese panel makers have shifted their focus from top-line growth to

profitability, while lower government subsidies have reinforced supply discipline. We
believe investors increasingly recognize that the industry's production-to-order model can
remain sustainable, particularly as panel price cycles shortened to quarters in 2023-24 vs.
multiyear cycles in the previous decade. Consolidation actions, including LG Display's sale
of its Gen 8.5 Guangzhou fab to TCL and Sharp's closure of its Gen 10 Sakai fab, also

support a healthier supply backdrop (for more information, see Display – Structural
Changes on Supply Side Not Fairly Priced In, June 17, 2021).

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Risk/Reward Largely Fair
We view risk-reward as broadly balanced following the recent correction in panel stocks.
Below are our current views:
• AUO (2409.TW, Derrick Yang): We rate AUO Equal-weight with a PT of NT
$27.00, based on a 1.4x 2026e P/B multiple. While we believe the current round of
TV panel price increases is nearing an end, major panel makers should continue to
demonstrate solid supply discipline, providing downside support. We appreciate

AUO's efforts to diversify its business, but meaningful earnings contributions are
likely to take time.
• BOE (000725.SZ, Derrick Yang): We prefer BOE due to its scale advantage,
higher exposure to display and faster progress in glass core substrates. Our PT of
Rmb9.30 is based on 2.5x 2026e P/B, which we view as reasonable given our
estimated ROE of 5-8% over 2026-28 compared with BOE's average P/B of 1.1x and
ROE of 2-4% during 2023-25.
• Innolux (3481.TW, Derrick Yang): In semiconductors, our supply-chain checks

suggest that Innolux is participating in a foundry customer's glass core substrate
project. However, we do not expect meaningful contributions before 2028, and
additional competitors could enter as the industry moves toward mass production.
We maintain our Equal-weight rating and PT of NT$60.00, based on a 2.1x 2026e
P/B multiple.
• LG Display (034220.KS, Ryan Kim): Meaningful OLED revenue contribution
should increasingly improve earnings from 2H25, but potentially weaker end-

markets keep us from turning bullish on LGD. The stock trades at 0.6x 2026e P/B
vs. a historical mid-cycle level of 0.5x.
• TCL (000100.SZ, Derrick Yang): We are EW with a price target of Rmb4.70 (1.7x
2026e P/B). The stock is trading at 1.7x 2026e P/B vs. its mid-cycle average of 1.4x
since 2022.
• Greater China DDIC supply chain implications – Novatek (3034.TW, Daniel Yen):
Our Underweight thesis reflects weaker PC, smartphone and automotive demand,

gross margin pressure, and a lack of new growth drivers. That said, Novatek's
OLED DDI roadmap appears more competitive than those of its key Korean peers
in terms of both performance and cost, helping it gain share in Apple's iPhone
supply chain.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Where We Could Be Wrong
• Stronger end demand: If end demand for major applications turns out to be better
than expected, there could be stronger panel shipments and less panel price
pressure.
• More stringent output control: If major panel makers collectively adjust
utilization to an extreme extent, they could mitigate pricing pressure in the case of
softer demand.
• Non-commodity display business: If the non-commodity business progresses

faster than expected, panel makers could show better financial results.

last 12 months. Due to rounding off of decimals, the percentages provided in the "% of total" column may not add up to exactly 100 percent.
Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over
the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

### Page 5

5

Kinsus Interconnect Technology (3189 TT): 6 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 31 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Kinsus's leadership team comprises experienced professionals dedicated to steering the
company towards sustainable growth. This diverse board composition ensures a comprehensive
range of expertise and perspectives, contributing to Kinsus’s strategic decision-making and
corporate governamce.
Capital management 2
Kinsus maintains a robust financial framework, ensuring the availability of resources necessary
for operational efficiency and strategic investments. The company's financial health is regularly
disclosed through annual reports, providing transparency to stakeholders.
Related party &
transaction 1
The company adheres to stringent corporate governance policies to manage related party
transactions, ensuring transparency and compliance with regulatory standards. The company
discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder
trust. For instance, the company's annual general meeting documents provide details on related
party transactions, ensuring transparency and accountability.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Kinsus is committed to sustainable supply chain practices, emphasizing environmental, social,
and governance considerations. The company has established comprehensive ESG policies,
including commitments to labor standards, environmental health and safety, ethics, and conflict-
free minerals. These policies guide the company's interactions with suppliers and partners,
promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.
E Data security 3
The company recognizes the importance of information security in its operations. As part of its
corporate governance framework, Kinsus is likely to implement standard data protection
practices to safeguard sensitive information and ensure compliance with relevant regulations.

company directly for detailed information on data security policies.

Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
September Sales -1% MoM/
+66% YoY
In this report, we focus on Accton's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September sales were NT$40,399mn (-1% MoM/+66% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue was NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY), in line with MSe
of NT$120,883mn (+27% QoQ/+66% YoY)  and consensus of NT$117.390mn
(+23% QoQ/+61% YoY) by 3%.
Our view:
• We believe the consignment arrangement of DRAM, PCB, etc., from August

might have capped the upside on revenue growth, but it should be a
favorable factor for gross margin, emerging from August/September and
likely extending into 4Q26.
• Driven by the network switch business and its major customer's AI
accelerator module ramp, we expect revenue to continue growing
sequentially in 4Q26, with the momentum in 2027 strengthened by potential
new customers and new products.
• Stay OW with a PT of NT$3,500, based on 30x 2027e P/E.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Update
Bizlink  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +5% MoM/
+39% YoY
In this report, we focus on Bizlink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales came in at NT$9,332mn (+5% MoM/+39% YoY)
• 3Q26 revenue reached NT$27,854mn (+20% QoQ/+50% YoY), slightly short
of MSe of NT$28,521mn (+23% QoQ/+55% YoY) by 2% while exceeding
consensus of NT$27,423mn (+18% QoQ/+49% YoY) by 2%.
Our view:
• We think 3Q26 revenue came in largely in-line with market expectations.

• Looking into 2H26, we expect HPC to remain the main growth driver, as
power interconnects should continue to see momentum from AI/general
servers. Meanwhile, data interconnects should grow along with new ASIC
model ramp-up and GPU server demand. Moreover, semi cap equipment
revenue growth should also sustain.
• We believe Bizlink has allocated increasing resources toward the optical
interconnect space, including the collaboration on ALC with Credo, mini

VCSEL optical module with ams-OSRAM and shuffle box with Corning. We
think that these opportunities could help offset a potential slowdown in its
AEC business as AEC solutions reach their scalability limits at higher data
rates, likely from  3.2T.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Bizlink (3665.TW)
Base case, derived from 30x 2027e P/E. We think our target P/E multiple is justified by an
earnings CAGR of 46% seen over 2025-28e, which implies a PEG of around 0.7. This is in line
with the average of 0.7 in 2019-24, when the stock was trading at an average 13x P/E for a 19%
earnings CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Faster and wider AEC adoption
n Data center and semi equipment capex increases that drive stronger HPC and semi-cap

business
n Stronger recovery for automotive business
Risks to Downside
n Lower-than-expected AEC adoption
n Intensifying competition
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower-than-expected growth in the semi capital equipment market
n Continued weakness in the auto industry

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部維持奇鋐買進投資建議，並上修目標價，主係：(1) 4Q26 隨著
VR200 小量出貨，AWS T3 水冷及Google TPU 同步放量，營收與獲利
將逐季走高；(2)預期 AWS 及 AMD 將採用雙面水冷版，單價大幅提升；
(3)ASIC 產品毛利率高於GPU， 且預估27 年水冷營收比重將超過 5 成；
(4) NVIDIA CDU 認證名單及 Switch tray 規格的市場雜音，對基本
面影響有限。綜上所述，研究部上修 27 年營收至 3,409 億元(原估
3,144 億元)，EPS 上修至 180.69 元(原估 162.71 元)，給予 25 倍本
益比，目標價上修至 4,500 元，維持買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 4Q隨著VR小批量出貨，兩家ASIC放量，營運持續季增 
隨著GB300出貨持續強勁 ，VR小批量出貨， 研究部預期奇鋐9月
營收持續月增。展望4Q26，除了VR200板端開始小量出貨外，
Google TPU以及AWS T3水冷也將在4Q開始陸續放量，研究部微
幅調整4Q26營收至632億元(原估622億元)，季增11.9%，年增
32.3%。而VR200的出貨將持續到2Q27，3Q27將開始有小量Rubin 
ultra產品接續。

2. AWS及AMD採用雙面水冷板，散熱產值增加 
根據供應鏈調查， AWS T3水冷以及AMD MI系列將採用雙面式
水冷板，主因採用背面供電(主板空間不夠，記憶體模組移到
主板背面，故兩面都需水冷板散熱)，雙面式水冷板單價將有
望超過3000美元，產值大幅提升。此外，ASIC產品毛利率優於
GPU，公司產能也優先配置給ASIC，故研究部上修27年營收至
3409億元。

3. CDU認證及switch tray規格雜音，對基本面影響極小 
近期散熱有部分雜音：(1) NVIDIA DSX Ready 的CDU認證裡，
取得資格的業者為 LG Electronics、LiquidStack與Vertiv三
間，奇鋐未在首批名單中。研究部認為，目前奇鋐目前主力產
品仍為水冷板等零組件，CDU比較偏向系統級產品 ，27-28年才
會開始小量生產CDU ( 以In row 架構為主)，但目前營收比重
低，影響不大。(2)傳言27年Rubin Ultra 的Switch tray將從
18層改回9層設計，目前仍在討論中，但研究部 對奇鋐的27年
EPS預估，本身就未納入18層規格。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

4. 上修27年EPS預估，目標價上修至4500元  
除了GPU及ASIC需要水冷板之外，周圍相關產品也開始逐漸導
入液冷，如CPU/LPU Rack 、CPO等，奇鋐預估27年水冷營收比
重將超過5成。綜上所述 ，研究部微幅上修2026年營收至2,178
億元(原估2,168億元)， 年增56.0%；EPS 104.65元(原估104.02
元)，並上修2027年營收至3,409億元(原估3,144億元)，年增
56.5%，EPS上修至180.69元(原估162.71元)，給予25倍本益比 ，
目標價上修至4,500元，維持買進建議。

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言 
© 2010 統 一 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 。 本 公 司 提 供 之 報 告 內 容 係 根 據 本 公
司 認 可 之 資 料來 源 ， 並 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 但 不 保 證 其 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 ， 報
告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

光環(3234) 
買進, TP 240 (潛在報酬 32.23%)  
買進 
Price(10/02):181.5 
Target Price：240

在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運， 隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化， 本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈 。 為配合客戶後續產能規劃， 公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充，預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證，有望切入新一代
AI 光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研
究部預估光環 2026 年 EPS 0.57 元，2027 年 EPS 6.77 元，目前
PE 為 26.5x，參考光通上游同業 PE 評價為 20-60x，考量光環本
業獲利將開始由虧轉盈且顯著成長，且也開始佈局 Scale-up 雷射
方案，因此給予 35xPER，目標價 240 元，建議買進。 
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
光環(3234)成立於 1997 年，主要產品為光通訊主動元件，包含雷
射晶粒切割、次模組封裝，2025 年晶粒及零組件佔 63%、光電轉
換件 28%、其他 9%。應用領域則為頻寬網路 10%、4G/5G 基地
台互聯 31%、雲端數據中心 22%、感測應用 26%、其他 11%，銷
售地區為台灣 60%、 中國8%、 歐洲及美洲17%、 亞洲及其他15%。

殖利率(%) - - ⚫ 營運概況 
取得客戶測試認證，代工業務重回貢獻 
光環 26Q2 營收 2.0 億元，Q+7.5%，Y+28.1%，毛利率 19.82%，
稅後淨利-1744 萬元，Q 持續虧損，Y 持續虧損，EPS -0.16 元。
在重新取得客戶 5000 小時的測試認證下，代工收入逐步重回貢獻
且產品組合優化下，使整體毛利率回升至雙位數以上，並在費用控
管得宜讓本業獲利虧損顯著收斂。

代工事業加速成長，本業由虧轉盈 
光環 8 月營收 1.1 億元，M+13.1%，Y+110.1%，7 月營收 9604
萬元，M+25.2%，Y+96.9%，7-8 月合計 2.0 億元，Y+103.7%。
代工事業逐月增加片數 ， 研究部認為在稼動率回升及產品組合優化
下，光環獲利將持續獲得顯著改善，其中7 月自結稅後淨利約 900
萬，EPS 0.08 元，反映出本業隨營收的放大而已轉虧為盈。綜合
以 上 ， 研 究 部 預 估 光 環26Q3 營收 3.2 億元， Q+63.9% ，
Y+113.2%，毛利率 33.77%，稅後淨利 4462 萬元，Q 虧轉盈，Y
虧轉盈，EPS 0.35 元。

產能積極布局，同時開發 1060nm VCSEL 自有產品 
光環今年以來已完成 16.5 億元現金增資(稀釋 EPS 約 11.8%)，並
公告將斥資約 11 億元進行產能擴充及設備擴置。研究部推估，此
次擴產主要係配合客戶 2027～2028 年產能規劃 ， 因而提前建置相
對應代工設備，預期至 2027 年底整體產能規模有望達 2026 年底
的 3 倍，且公司亦已著手規劃 2028 年後續擴產計畫。綜上所述，
預期光環代工業務將自 26H2 起加速成長，隨產品組合改善，本業
可望轉為穩定獲利，加以產能利用率提升帶動營運槓桿逐步顯現，
預期整體獲利增幅將優於營收成長。此外，光環深耕VCSEL 市場
多 年 ， 而 今 年 以 來 多 家 國 際 光 通 訊 及 雷 射 大 廠 亦 陸 續 提 出 以

VCSEL 作為 Scale-up 光互連光源的方案 。 相較SiPh 方案 ，VCSEL
具備高頻寬密度、低功耗、低延遲潛力及高可靠度等優勢，過往傳
輸 距 離 較 短 的 限 制 ， 在 以 機 櫃 內 及 機 櫃 間 短 距 離 互 連 為 主 的
Scale-up 應用中影響亦相對有限。研究部認為，若未來 Scale-up
架構朝 Wide-and-Slow 方向發展，VCSEL 將成為極具競爭力的技
術選項。光環憑藉既有技術與量產經驗，目前也開始佈局1060nm
的 VCSEL 方案，預計於年底推出樣品給客戶測試，未來有望成為
強化公司長期競爭優勢的自有產品。綜合以上，研究部預估光環
2026 年營收 11.0 億元 ，Y+60.7%， 毛利率28.29%， 稅後淨利7233
萬元 ，Y 虧轉盈 ，EPS 0.57 元；2027 年營收 28.8 億元 ，Y+162.0%，
毛利率 40.89%，稅後淨利 8.6 億元，Y+1083.9%，EPS 6.77 元。

結論 
在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運，隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化，本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈。為配合客戶後續產能規劃，公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充， 預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證 ， 有望切入新一代AI
光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研究部

漲價紅利延續；特殊材料蓄勢待發 
 漲價不斷，2H26 獲利將持續跳升：2H26 營運動能持續增溫，主要
受惠 CCL 缺貨，不斷漲價，獲利預期出現跳升。M6 以下、無鉛、
無鹵素及高 TG 等一般材料訂單外溢至騰輝，騰輝 2026 年一般材料
價格 YTD+50%，一般材料已貢獻公司獲利超過一半，9 月份一般材
料再續漲 10~15%；且一般材料產品組合持續轉佳，8 月份起單價與
獲利率較佳之高 TG 產品出貨量成長超過一倍 ， 基本上目前公司所有
產品線毛利率皆達 30%以上，至於特殊材料仍穩定貢獻。 
 深化特殊材料布局 ， 期待新專案驅動中長線成長：截至 3Q26 VT-901
產品營收+70~80%YOY，除了軍工需求倍增，亦有數家國際半導體
設備大廠將 VT-901 用於半導體老化測試板。近期騰輝新增 8～9 個
散熱專案，拓展至 AI 與高功率電源散熱應用，提供後續特殊材料成
長動能。高速材料方面，騰輝亦有送樣下一世代 AI 專案(碳氫 CCL
與 PTFE 皆有送樣)，期待成為 AI 終端客戶二供廠商。 
 買進評等：預期一般材料漲價效益可延續至 2027 年，且若新專案驗
證順利，有望進一步推升產品組合。預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 分別為
15.12/30.86 元，評等買進，目標價 460 元(15x2027EPS)。

個股報告 
騰輝電子-KY 產品主要分一般材
料與特殊材料

騰輝電子-KY 主要從事印刷電路板所需之黏合片、銅箔 基板及鋁基板生產與銷
售，一般材料產品應用於光電板、汽車板等中低階產品，特殊材料主要為散熱
鋁基板，另外亦有著墨軍工航天等利基型領域， 2025 年產品組合：一般材料
41%、特殊材料 53%、其他 6%；2026 年一般材料受惠漲價、缺貨，預估2026
年產品組合：一般材料 53%、特殊材料 41%、其他 6%。騰輝總部位於中國蘇
州，主要生產基地位亦位於中國蘇州(月產能 80 萬張)、江陰(月產能 20 萬張)、
深圳(月產能 10 萬張)與台灣(月產能 10 萬張)， 泰國廠(月產能 10 萬張)則於 2026
年 9 月開始量產。

漲價不斷，2H26 獲利將持續跳升  騰輝 2H26 營運動能持續增溫，主要受惠 CCL 缺貨，不斷漲價，獲利預期出現
跳升。CCL 上游原料方面，E 布維持逐月漲價，而銅箔供給緊張程度僅次於玻
纖布；CCL 方面，CCL 龍頭廠商將產能集中於 M6 以上高速材料，中低階 CCL
供給明顯收縮，M6 以下、無鉛、無鹵素及高 TG 等一般材料訂單外溢至騰輝，
騰輝 2026 年一般材料價格 YTD+50%，一般材料已貢獻公司獲利超過一半，9
月份一般材料再續漲 10~15%；且一般材料產品組合持續轉佳，8 月份起單價與
獲利率較佳之高 TG 產品出貨量成長超過一倍 ， 基本上目前公司所有產品線毛利
率皆達 30%以上，至於特殊材料仍穩定貢獻；綜上所述，預估3Q26/4Q26 毛利
率將分別提升至 39.8%/41.6%， 推估3Q26/4Q26 EPS 分別為 4.38 元/6.04 元，
合計推估 2026 全年 EPS 為 15.12 元。

深化特殊材料布局，期待新專案
驅動中長線成長 
 騰輝長期聚焦利基型特殊材料市場，並具備自行合成樹脂能力，除了掌握關鍵
配 方 外 ， 亦 可 降 低 原 料 成 本 。PI 方 面 ， 截 至 3Q26 V T-901 產 品 營 收
+70~80%YOY，除了軍工需求倍增，亦有數家國際半導體設備大廠將 VT-901
用於半導體老化測試板，PI 產品毛利率約有 70%高水準；其他高毛利率產品如
不流膠 PP 營收亦有好表現，截至 3Q26 不流膠 PP 營收+50%YOY。另外，騰
輝為全球主要車燈散熱材料供應商，其散熱鋁基板 80%應用於汽車照明，近期
騰輝新增 8～9 個散熱專案，拓展至 AI 與高功率電源散熱應用，提供後續特殊
材料成長動能。高速材料方面，騰輝亦有送樣下一世代 AI 專案(碳氫 CCL 與
PTFE 皆有送樣)，期待成為 AI 終端客戶二供廠商。產能方面，目前稼動率水準
未達滿載以提供訂單彈性；泰國新廠今年 9 月開始試加工，初期由裁切等後段
製程開始，上膠機預計 1H27 進廠裝機，完整產線預計 2027 年到位，目前規劃
至少兩條產線，若全產能運行，預估可增加 10～15%產能，除支應既有客戶需
求外，亦為 2027 年新產品放量預留成長空間；此外，騰輝亦通過 15 億元昆山
擴建案，新產能亦預計於 2027 年展開貢獻。

治療藥物將取證，AB-BNCT 邁向商業化階段 
 B10 L-BPA 治療藥物 2026 年底取得台灣藥證 ：禾榮科 AB-BNCT 設
備已於 2024 年 7 月正式取得 TFDA 醫療器材許可證 ， 其核心治療新
藥 B10 L -BPA 之復發型頭頸癌已完成收案，主要評估指標為治療後
三個月的客觀反應率（ORR） ，預計2026 年底取得藥證，取得藥證
後 ， 將可正式收治病患 ， 且未來將擴大適應症至三陰性乳癌與肺腺癌 。 
 1H27 年北榮 BNCT 機台將完成驗收：禾榮科積極布局國內外市場，
在國內已與中國醫藥大學新竹附設醫院展開產學合作， 2024 年 11
月正式啟動臨床收案；臺北榮民總醫院的建置工程則於 2025 年 8 月
動工，預計 1Q27 完工，1H27 可認列新台幣 12 億元營收；海外方
面，佳世達集團旗下的南京明基醫院斥資 5500 萬美元採購設備，同
時禾榮科技攜手新加坡國立大學醫院共建研發樞紐 ， 成功將台灣自主
研發的 AB-BNCT 癌症治療技術推向國際舞台。 
 禾榮科治療藥物即將取得許可證，屆時即可正式開始收費，同時，
1H27 將認列台北榮總 BNCT 設備 12 億元營收 ，2027 年開始轉虧為

科投資評等維持買進，預估 2027 年 EPS 6.29 元，目標價 700 元。

個股報告 
禾 榮 科 核 心 技 術 為 自 主 研 發
的 AB -BNCT 癌症治療設備  
 禾榮科成立於 2017 年，專注於發展先進的癌症治療技術，公司核心業務為加
速器型硼中子捕獲治療（Accelerator-Based Boron Neut ron Capture Therapy, 
AB-BNCT），此項技術源自清華大學與工業技術研究院歷經三十年的研究成
果，禾榮科成功將這項學研基礎轉化為商業應用，提供完整的 AB-BNCT 整體
解決方案，涵蓋質子加速器系統、中子源靶系統、中子束調整器、治療控制系
統、治療計畫系統以及病人定位系統等關鍵設備，致力於為醫療機構與患者帶
來精準、高效的治療選擇。

B10 L -BPA 治 療 藥 物 預 計
2026 年底取得台灣藥證  
 禾榮科技 AB-BNCT 設備已於 2024 年 7 月正式取得 TFDA 醫療器材許可證，其
核心治療新藥 B10 L-BPA (10B-L-Boronophenylalanine)正進行復發型頭頸癌、
惡性腦瘤及復發型腦膜瘤的臨床試驗，復發型頭頸癌收案 24 人，主要評估指標
為治療後三個月的客觀反應率 （ORR） ，預計2026 年底取得藥證，目前禾榮科
營收仍主要來自恩慈治療，取得藥證後，將可正式收治病患，且未來將擴大適
應症至三陰性乳癌與肺腺癌等；此外，搭配術前精準評估劑量的 F18 FBPA 診
斷用藥已於 4Q25 進入臨床試驗。
